  "abstract_verbatim": "For every fixed degree <i>d</i> ≥ 3, we prove fixed-energy GOE universality for the adjacency matrix of a uniformly random simple labelled <i>d</i>-regular graph with independent uniform diagonal disorder. The disorder strength is positive, sufficiently small, and fixed as the graph grows. <a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every fixed degree <i>d</i> ≥ 3, we prove fixed-energy GOE universality for the adjacency matrix of a uniformly random simple labelled <i>d</i>-regular graph with independent uniform diagonal disorder. The disorder strength is positive, sufficiently small, and fixed as the graph grows. At each fixed energy in a compact subinterval of the clean spectral band, the full microscopic eigenvalue point process converges to the GOE bulk process after rescaling by the positive density of states of the corresponding infinite-tree operator. The permitted disorder strength depends on the degree and the distance from the clean spectral edges.

- **claim-main-universality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every $d\ge3$ and every $0<\kappa<2\sqrt{d-1}$, there is $w_0=w_0(d,\kappa)>0$ with the following property. For each fixed $0<w<w_0$, the restriction of $\nu_{d,w}$ to an open neighborhood of $$I_{d,\kappa}=[-2\sqrt{d-1}+\kappa,\,2\sqrt{d-1}-\kappa]$$ has a continuous density $\rho_{d,w}$, strictly positive on $I_{d,\kappa}$. For every fixed $E\in I_{d,\kappa}$, set $$\Xi_{n,E}=\sum_{i=1}^n \delta_{n\rho_{d,w}(E)(\lambda_i-E)}.$$ Then, for every nonnegative $f\in C_c(\mathbb R)$, $$\label{eq:main-laplace} \left\| \mathbb E\exp\left(-\int f\,d\Xi_{n,E}\right) -\mathbb E\exp\left(-\int f\,d\Xi_n^{\mathrm{GOE}}\right) \right\|\longrightarrow0$$ as $n\to\infty$ through all sizes $n>d$ with $nd$ even.
  - Explanation: For weak fixed disorder on a random regular graph of fixed degree at least three, the microscopic eigenvalue process at a fixed bulk energy, unfolded by the tree density of states, has the same Laplace functionals in the limit as the GOE bulk process.
- **claim-tree-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-tree-stability)): There is an integer $\ell\ge1$, depending only on $b,J$, with the following properties. Given any finite order $p_*>0$ and a sufficiently small prescribed number $\delta_{\rm tree}>0$, one can choose $h,w_0>0$ so that, uniformly for $e\in J$, $0<w\le w_0$, and $0<\eta\le h$, $$\label{eq:tree-small-moments} \\|Y(e,\eta)\\|_2\le\delta_{\rm tree},\qquad \mathbb ED(Y(e,\eta))^p\le K_p\quad(0<p\le p_*).$$ The choices also include the additional fixed finite moment orders required by the stability assertion that follows. There are constants $\delta_*>0$ and $C<\infty$ such that, if a probability law $P$ on $\mathbb D$ satisfies $\int\|x\|^2\,dP(x)\le\delta_*^2$ and $$W_2\bigl(P,\mathop{\mathrm{Law}}(F_j(X_1,\ldots,X_{b^j}))\bigr)\le u, \qquad j=1,\ell,$$ where the $X_i$ are independent with law $P$, then $$\label{eq:tree-law-stability} W_2(P,\mathop{\mathrm{Law}}(Y(e,\eta)))\le Cu.$$ The constants $K_p,\delta_*,C$ can be fixed before decreasing the parameters further to make $\delta_{\rm tree}$ much smaller than $\delta_*$. On a fixed tree with its fixed potential variables, $(e,\eta)\mapsto Y(e,\eta)$ extends continuously in $L^2$ to $J\times[0,h]$. The boundary values belong to $\mathbb D$ almost surely, obey <a href="#eq:tree-small-moments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tree-small-moments">[eq:tree-small-moments]</a>, and are measurable functions of the potentials.
  - Explanation: The cavity Green function recursion on the forward tree is stable near the clean bulk solution: boundary values are small in the disk coordinate, have inverse-margin moments, and any approximate fixed-point law is close to the true tree law. Boundary values exist continuously in the common environment.
- **claim-tree-covariance-log-integrability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tree-log)): Fix a finite exponent $p_0\ge1$ before choosing the disorder threshold. Taking the finite order $p_*$ in Proposition <a href="#prop:tree-stability" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:tree-stability">2</a> sufficiently large, the full-root Green function and every entry of the star Green matrix extend continuously in $L^{p_0}$ to $J\times[0,h]$ on the common environment. Their boundary values have strictly positive root imaginary part. If $K(\zeta)$ is the imaginary part of the Green matrix compressed to an edge or a retained star, then $K(e+i0)$ is positive definite almost surely. Uniformly for $e\in J$ and $0\le\eta\le h$, the variables $\log\det K(e+i\eta)$ are uniformly integrable, and they converge in $L^1$ when $(e,\eta)$ converges. These assertions require only a finite collection of inverse-margin moments of spatial cavity variables, independent of the number of colors in a Gaussian field.
  - Explanation: Star and edge Green matrices extend continuously to the boundary in a finite Lebesgue-type norm, their imaginary-part covariances are positive definite, and the log-determinants are uniformly integrable using only finitely many inverse-margin moments.
- **claim-tree-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-tree-density)): For the above smallness choices, the restriction of $\nu_{d,w}$ to the interior of $J$ has the continuous strictly positive density $$\label{eq:tree-density} \rho_{d,w}(e)=\frac1\pi\mathbb E\Im m_o(e+i0).$$ In particular this holds on an open neighborhood of $I_{d,\kappa}$ when that interval lies in the interior of $J$.
  - Explanation: The tree density-of-states measure has a continuous strictly positive density on the interior of the bulk interval, given by the expected imaginary part of the root Green function at the boundary.
- **claim-distributional-local-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-local-law)): There are $c,C>0$ and $y_0<\infty$ such that, for $y=n\eta\ge y_0$, $$\label{eq:distributional-local-law} \mathbb P\left\{ W_2(P_H,Q_\zeta)+W_2(P_H^0,Q_\zeta^0)>y^{-c} \right\}\le Cy^{-c}.$$ Here $W_2$ is transportation distance for the Euclidean metric on the disk. The estimate is uniform in $e\in J$ and $0<\eta\le h$.
  - Explanation: With high probability the empirical laws of cavity and full-root resolvents are close in transportation distance to the tree laws, with power losses in the product of graph size and spectral height.
- **claim-spectral-averaging** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-spectral-averaging)): Let $H=A+\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(V_v)$, where $A$ is a real symmetric matrix independent of the iid uniform potentials $V_v\in[-w,w]$. For $\Im\zeta>0$, $$\label{eq:site-averaging} \mathbb E\,\Im G_{vv}(\zeta)\le C_w, \qquad \mathbb E\,\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\mathbf1_I(H)\le C_w n\|I\|$$ for every interval $I\subset\mathbb R$. If $S$ consists of at most $k$ vertices chosen without reference to the potentials, then $$\label{eq:compression-tail} \mathbb P\{\\|G_{SS}(\zeta)\\|>D\}\le C_{w,k}D^{-1/2},\qquad D\ge1.$$ The same conclusions hold after any graph modifications chosen independently of the potentials. The compression estimate also holds for the inverse of a fixed strictly dissipative $k\times k$ matrix plus an iid uniform real diagonal; its constant is independent of that matrix.
  - Explanation: Averaging over independent uniform potentials bounds expected diagonal resolvent imaginary parts and eigenvalue counts, and gives a power tail for compressions to a bounded number of vertices.
- **claim-sampled-ward** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ward)): Let $v,v'$ be independent uniform vertices, conditionally on $H$. Then $$\label{eq:sampled-ward} \mathbb E_{H,v,v'}\|G_{vv'}(\zeta)\|^2 =\frac1{n\eta}\mathbb E\,\Im\left(\frac1n\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}G(\zeta)\right) \le\frac{C_w}{y}.$$ The same estimate applies to vertices read at prescribed positions of two independently sampled oriented edges or finite ordered nonbacktracking tree explorations.
  - Explanation: The mean square of an off-diagonal resolvent entry between independently sampled vertices is controlled by the normalized imaginary trace divided by the product of size and height, hence small.
- **claim-empirical-recursion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-empirical-recursion)): For each fixed $j\ge1$ there are $c,C,y_0>0$ such that, when $y\ge y_0$, $$\label{eq:empirical-recursion} \mathbb P\{W_2(P_H,\Phi_j(P_H))>y^{-c}\}\le Cy^{-c}.$$ The same assertion holds with $P_H^0$ on the left and $\Phi_j^0(P_H)$ on the right. Both assertions also hold for the augmented observations just defined. Probabilities in these estimates average over the graph and potentials; the measures being compared are conditional laws obtained by sampling on the realized $H$.
  - Explanation: Via marked edge switches, the empirical cavity law of the finite graph nearly satisfies the independent-branch recursion, also for full-root and augmented observations.
- **claim-local-consequences** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-local-consequences)): Fix $E$ in the interior of $J$ and $s\in\mathbb R$. The following statements hold.* 1. *At $\zeta=E+(s+iy)/n$, in the order $n\to\infty$ and then $y\to\infty$, the diagonal $G_{vv}(\zeta)$ at a uniform vertex, jointly with every fixed ordered neighborhood of potentials around $v$, converges to the tree diagonal $m_o(E+i0)$ with the corresponding tree environment. For any fixed number of independently sampled vertices the joint assertion holds with independent tree environments, and their off-diagonal Green entries tend to zero in probability. The diagonal assertion also holds along every sequence $n\eta\to\infty$, $(e,\eta)\to(E,0)$.* 2. *There are $c,C>0$ such that, for every sufficiently large fixed $y$, $$\label{eq:trace-slow-power} \limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb P\left\{ \left\|\Im\frac1n\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}G\left(E+\frac{s+iy}{n}\right) -\pi\rho_{d,w}(E)\right\|>y^{-c}\right\}\le Cy^{-c}.$$ The constants are independent of the fixed real number $s$.* 3. *For each fixed $s\in\mathbb R$ and $y>0$, the sampled diagonal imaginary parts are uniformly integrable in the following limiting sense: $$\label{eq:microscopic-ui} \lim_{T\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty} \mathbb E_{H,v}\left[ \Im G_{vv}\left(E+\frac{s+iy}{n}\right) \mathbf1_{\{\Im G_{vv}(E+(s+iy)/n)>T\}}\right]=0.$$* *The double-limit assertion in the first item means that, for bounded continuous tests, the limsup of the discrepancy as $n\to\infty$ tends to zero as $y\to\infty$.
  - Explanation: The local law yields convergence of sampled diagonal resolvents to the tree diagonal with its environment, concentration of the normalized imaginary trace around the tree density, and microscopic uniform integrability of diagonal imaginary parts.
- **claim-marked-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-marked-limits)): Every sequence of admissible sizes tending to infinity has a subsequence on which $\nu_n$ converges in law to a locally finite point measure $\nu$. On a further subsequence one can retain jointly the positions and the empirical marked-neighborhood laws for the eigenvalue-ordered lists in an exhaustion by bounded intervals, together with all their finite truncations and restrictions. Repeated limiting positions retain separate marks.* *Almost every resulting empirical law $\mathcal L$, with $m$ colors, is an automorphism-invariant law on the $d$-regular tree, whose potential marginal is the deterministic iid uniform law on $[-w,w]$. If $U_v\in\mathbb R^m$ is its mark at $v$, then $$\label{eq:mark-normalization-equation} \mathbb E_{\mathcal L}U_oU_o^{\mathsf T}=\mathrm I_m, \qquad (V_v-E)U_v+\sum_{u\sim v}U_u=0\quad\text{at every }v.$$ The empirical laws and their restrictions to smaller lists can be chosen consistently.
  - Explanation: Along subsequences, positions and empirical marked-neighborhood laws converge; almost every limit is an automorphism-invariant tree law with deterministic iid potentials, unit mark covariance and the tree eigen-equation.
- **claim-conditional-centering** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-conditional-mean)): For almost every empirical law in Proposition <a href="#prop:marked-limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:marked-limits">10</a>, $$\label{eq:conditional-zero-mean} \mathbb E_{\mathcal L}[U_v\mid\omega]=0 \qquad\text{at every vertex }v.$$
  - Explanation: A small mesoscopic potential perturbation averages out coherent environment-dependent components, so conditional means of limiting marks given the environment vanish.
- **claim-conditional-entropy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-conditional-entropy)): For almost every empirical law $\mathcal L$ with $m\ge1$ colors, and for every fixed height in any prescribed countable set of positive heights, $$\label{eq:conditional-star-edge-entropy} \begin{split} &\mathcal H(F^\eta_{\rm star}\mid\omega) -\frac d2\mathcal H(F^\eta_{\rm edge}\mid\omega)\\ &\hspace{1cm}\ge m\left[\frac12\log(2\pi\mathrm e\,\eta) -\int\log\|t-E-i\eta\|\,d\nu_{d,w}(t)\right]. \end{split}$$
  - Explanation: For Gaussian-smoothed marks, the conditional entropy of a star minus half the sum over edges is bounded below by a quantity involving the logarithmic potential of the tree density of states.
- **claim-star-edge-precision** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tree-precision)): The centered Gaussian with covariance $K_o$ is supported on the $d$-dimensional hyperplane $$\label{eq:star-constraint} C:=(V_o-E)Z+\sum_{i=1}^dX_i=0.$$ In the coordinate volume $\delta(C)\,dZ\prod_i dX_i$, equivalently the volume obtained by forgetting any one neighbor, its precision quadratic form is $$\label{eq:star-precision} \sum_{i=1}^d\frac{\|X_i+g_iZ\|^2}{b_i}.$$ Write $K_o^{\rm ret}$ for its covariance in coordinates forgetting neighbor $d$. Then $$\label{eq:star-determinant} \det K_o^{\rm ret}=\|m_o\|^2\prod_{i=1}^d b_i, \qquad h_o^{\rm G}:=\mathcal H(N(0,K_o^{\rm ret})) =\frac d2\log(2\pi\mathrm e)+\frac12\sum_i\log b_i+\log\|m_o\|.$$ If $g'$ is the cavity message from the $o$-side of the edge $(o,i)$, its edge precision and determinant are $$\begin{aligned} (Z,X_i)K_{oi}^{-1}(Z,X_i)^{\mathsf T} &=\frac{\|X_i+g_iZ\|^2}{b_i} +\frac{\|Z+g'X_i\|^2}{\Im g'},\label{eq:edge-precision}\\ \det K_{oi}&=\frac{b_i\Im g'}{\|1-g_i g'\|^2}. \label{eq:edge-determinant} \end{aligned}$$ In particular each edge covariance is positive definite.
  - Explanation: The boundary Gaussian on a star lives on a constraint hyperplane with explicit precision, determinant and entropy, and every edge covariance is positive definite with precision splitting into two endpoint contributions.
- **claim-tree-log-potential** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tree-log-potential)): At positive height, with all tree Green functions evaluated at $\zeta$, $$\label{eq:tree-log-potential} -U(\zeta)=\mathbb E_\omega\log\|m_o\| +\frac d2\mathbb E_\omega\log\|1-g_i g'\|.$$ Both sides have finite limits as $\zeta=E+i\eta$, $\eta\downarrow0$. Denote the limit of $U$ by $U(E)$. With $h_{oi}^{\rm G}=\mathcal H(N(0,K_{oi}))$, $$\label{eq:gaussian-entropy-constant} \mathbb E_\omega\left[h_o^{\rm G}-\frac12\sum_{i=1}^d h_{oi}^{\rm G}\right] =-U(E).$$
  - Explanation: The logarithmic potential of the tree density of states equals an expected environment quantity built from root and edge factors, has finite boundary limits, and fixes the Gaussian entropy constant.
- **claim-gaussian-marks** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-gaussian-marks)): Within almost every empirical law $\mathcal L$ of Proposition <a href="#prop:marked-limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:marked-limits">10</a>, the conditional root marks have law $$\label{eq:conditional-gaussian-marks} \mathop{\mathrm{Law}}_{\mathcal L}(U_o\mid\omega) =N\left(0,\frac{\Im m_o(E+i0)}{\pi\rho}\mathrm I_m\right).$$ In particular the colors are conditionally independent. For any finite collection of independently sampled roots and any retained finite position list, their environments are iid and independent of the limiting point process $\nu$. Conditional on these environments and $\nu$, all their marks are independent centered Gaussians with the variances in <a href="#eq:conditional-gaussian-marks" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:conditional-gaussian-marks">[eq:conditional-gaussian-marks]</a>. Positions of multiplicity greater than one have one separate color per occurrence.
  - Explanation: Equality in the entropy comparison forces the conditional root marks to be independent centered Gaussians whose variance is set by the imaginary part of the tree Green function over the density.
- **claim-count-discrepancy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-discrepancy)): There is a deterministic $\chi>0$ such that, almost surely, $$\label{eq:discrepancy} \nu([v,u])=\rho(u-v)+O(R^{1-\chi}), \qquad -R\le v\le u\le R,\qquad R\longrightarrow\infty,$$ uniformly in $v,u$. The implicit constant may depend on the realization. The same conclusion holds with any endpoint convention. In particular, $$\label{eq:position-tail-bounds} \mathop{\mathrm{PV}}\sum_{\|x_j\|>R}\frac1{x_j}=O(R^{-\chi}),\qquad \sum_{\|x_j\|>R}\frac1{x_j^2}=O(R^{-1}).$$ Here and below $\mathop{\mathrm{PV}}$ means symmetric truncation in the position: $\lim_{L\to\infty}\sum_{\|x_j\|\le L}$.
  - Explanation: The limiting point measure has a power-saving count discrepancy relative to constant density on large intervals, which gives tail bounds for principal-value and inverse-square sums of positions.
- **claim-marked-resolvent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-marked-resolvent)): Almost surely, for every finite $k$ and every $z\in\mathbb H$, $$\label{eq:marked-resolvent} M_k(z)=A_k+\mathop{\mathrm{PV}}\sum_j\frac{b_jb_j^{\mathsf T}}{x_j-z}.$$ The principal values converge locally uniformly. Moreover, $\Im M_k(z)$ is positive definite, simultaneously for all these $k,z$.
  - Explanation: The marked resolvent compression equals a real diagonal constant from the tree environments plus a principal-value spectral sum with Gaussian weights, and its imaginary part is positive definite.
- **claim-transform-regularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-transform-regularity)): There is a deterministic $\xi>0$ such that, almost surely, for every fixed $0<\delta<1$, $$\label{eq:transform-regularity} m(e+iy)=i\pi\rho+O(y^{-\xi}), \qquad \|e\|\le R,\quad R^\delta\le y\le R^2, \qquad R\longrightarrow\infty.$$ The implicit constant may depend on $\delta$ and the realization.
  - Explanation: Using one-site potential resampling, the scalar principal-value transform is uniformly close to a constant multiple of the imaginary unit on growing regions at polynomial heights above the real axis.
- **claim-potential-switch** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-potential-switch)): Fix $k$. At site $\alpha\le k$, fix a measure-preserving map $T_\alpha:[-w,w]\to[-w,w]$ and an event $B_\alpha$ measurable in its root-excluded tree environment. Set $$D_\alpha=\boldsymbol1_{B_\alpha} \bigl(T_\alpha(V_\alpha)-V_\alpha\bigr), \qquad D=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(D_1,\ldots,D_k).$$ Then, for every finite set of arguments in $\mathbb H$, the imaginary parts of $$\label{eq:potential-switch-transform} m(z)-\partial_z\log\det(\mathrm I+D M_k(z))$$ have jointly the same law as the corresponding imaginary parts of $m(z)$. The identity is unconditional. In particular it applies to piecewise translations and to arbitrary measurable root-excluded selections of whether to perform them.
  - Explanation: Measure-preserving potential changes selected by the root-excluded environment leave unchanged the joint law of the relevant imaginary parts of a transform built from a finite-rank determinant derivative.
- **claim-gaussian-insertion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-gaussian-insertion)): On the preceding extension of any microscopic subsequential law, let $\mathcal W_r$ be the upper-left $m_I$-dimensional block of an infinite raw GOE array independent of $\mathcal E$, and set $$\label{eq:insertion-matrix} H_r=D_I+s_1\mathrm I_{m_I}+\sqrt{s_2}\,\mathcal W_r.$$ Then, along $r=2^j$, the raw eigenvalue counting measures $$\nu^{(r)}=\sum_{j=1}^{m_I}\delta_{\lambda_j(H_r)} \quad\Longrightarrow\quad\nu$$ converge in law for the local vague topology, without conditioning. Moreover $m_I=2\rho K+O(K^{1-\chi})$ almost surely, and the coefficient bounds <a href="#eq:insertion-coefficients" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:insertion-coefficients">[eq:insertion-coefficients]</a> hold eventually almost surely.
  - Explanation: Every microscopic subsequential law is the limit of eigenvalue measures of a finite diagonal matrix plus a scalar shift plus GOE noise, with control of the matrix dimension and coefficients.
- **claim-weighted-block-estimates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sw-weighted-blocks)): For $\mathcal A=I,F$, put $a_I=h_1$, $a_F=K$. With probability tending to one, $$\label{eq:sw-bernstein} \left\\|U_{\mathcal A} (A'_{\mathcal A\mathcal A}-s_1\mathrm I) U_{\mathcal A}\right\\| \le \operatorname{polylog}(r) \left(\sqrt{T/a_{\mathcal A}}+\theta\right).$$ Moreover, for $P_0=U_IA'_{IF}U_F$, $$\label{eq:sw-cross-block} \\|P_0\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\le T\operatorname{polylog}(r)$$ with probability tending to one.
  - Explanation: Weighted norms of the inner and far diagonal blocks of the Gaussian perturbation, and the Hilbert–Schmidt norm of the cross block, are controlled with high probability by matrix concentration.
- **claim-far-block-schur** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sw-schur)): Let $$\delta_F=\operatorname{polylog}(r) \left(\frac{1+T}{K}+\sqrt{\frac TK}+\theta\right).$$ Then $$\label{eq:sw-far-resolvent} \\|U_F^{-1}(G_F-D_F^{-1})U_F^{-1}\\|\le\delta_F=o(1)$$ with probability tending to one, and $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}G_F=o_{\mathbb P}(1)$. For the Schur correction $\Sigma$, $$\label{eq:sw-schur-small} \\|U_I\Sigma U_I\\|=o_{\mathbb P}(T^{-5}).$$
  - Explanation: The far-block resolvent is close to the inverse of its diagonal in weighted norm, its trace is negligible, and the Schur correction back on the inner block is very small.
- **claim-inner-resolvent-trace-cut** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sw-trace-cut)): For every fixed $z\in\mathbb H$, $$\label{eq:sw-inner-resolvent} \\|U_I^{-1}G_I(z)U_I^{-1}\\|=O_{\mathbb P}(h_1),$$ and $$\label{eq:sw-second-cut} \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(D_x+A'-z)^{-1} =\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(D_I+A'_{II}-z)^{-1}+o_{\mathbb P}(1).$$ The same estimates hold jointly for a fixed finite list of $z$’s.
  - Explanation: The inner resolvent has a controlled weighted norm and the trace of the full resolvent equals that of the inner block up to a vanishing error.
- **claim-gaussian-replacement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sw-lindeberg)): Conditional on almost every realization of $\mathcal E$, bounded smooth tests with bounded derivatives of finitely many trace values have expectation difference $o(1)$ between $$D_I+A'_{II} \quad\hbox{and}\quad H_r=D_I+s_1\mathrm I_{m_I}+\sqrt{s_2}\,\mathcal W.$$
  - Explanation: By a Lindeberg replacement, the sum of independent rank-one Gaussian terms may be replaced by a GOE matrix with matched mean and variance at vanishing cost for smooth trace tests.
- **claim-dbm-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-dbm-input)): Suppose a sequence of deterministic diagonal matrices $V$ of dimension $m\to\infty$ satisfies <a href="#eq:dbm-regularity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dbm-regularity">[eq:dbm-regularity]</a> and <a href="#eq:dbm-parameter-domain" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dbm-parameter-domain">[eq:dbm-parameter-domain]</a>, with fixed constants. Fix $\sigma>0$, and suppose $$\label{eq:dbm-time-window} g m^\sigma\le t\le m^{-\sigma}G^2.$$ Assume also that $\rho_{\mathrm{fc},t}(0)=1/\pi$. For every fixed positive integer $a$ and $O\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^a)$, the difference $$\begin{aligned} \label{eq:dbm-marginal-test} &\int_{\mathbb R^a}O(\boldsymbol\alpha) p_{V+\sqrt t\,W_m}^{(a)} \left(\frac{\pi\alpha_1}{m},\ldots, \frac{\pi\alpha_a}{m}\right) \,d\boldsymbol\alpha \notag\\ &\hspace{12mm}- \int_{\mathbb R^a}O(\boldsymbol\alpha) p_{W_m}^{(a)} \left(\frac{\pi\alpha_1}{m},\ldots, \frac{\pi\alpha_a}{m}\right) \,d\boldsymbol\alpha \longrightarrow0. \end{aligned}$$ Consequently, the expected sums of $O$ over ordered tuples of distinct points of the two eigenvalue processes, both rescaled by $m/\pi$, differ by $o(1)$.
  - Explanation: For regular deterministic diagonal data and a suitable time window, adding GOE noise gives local correlation statistics matching those of GOE at the normalized density, specialising a known fixed-energy result.
- **claim-density-adjustment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-density-adjustment)): For almost every realization of $\mathcal E$, the matrices $V_*$ are eventually regular with $$g=\frac{T^{1/2}}{m_I},\qquad G=m_I^{-1/8}.$$ They satisfy the hypotheses <a href="#eq:dbm-parameter-domain" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dbm-parameter-domain">[eq:dbm-parameter-domain]</a> and <a href="#eq:dbm-time-window" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:dbm-time-window">[eq:dbm-time-window]</a> with $m=m_I$ and $\delta=\sigma=\tau$. Moreover, $$\rho_{\mathrm{fc},t_*}(0)\longrightarrow\frac1\pi.$$ If $c_r=\pi\rho_{\mathrm{fc},t_*}(0)$, then eventually $c_r>0$, and the data $c_rV_*$ and time $c_r^2t_*$ satisfy Theorem <a href="#thm:dbm-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:dbm-input">25</a>, including its exact density condition.
  - Explanation: The rescaled diagonal data are regular in the required parameter window, and a dilation tending to one normalizes the free-convolution density at zero exactly, so the DBM input applies.
- **claim-goe-count-moments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-goe-counts)): For every compact interval $J\subset\mathbb R$ and every $b>0$, $$\sup_{m\ge1}\mathbb E\exp\bigl(b\,\mathcal G_m(J)\bigr)<\infty.$$
  - Explanation: Exponential moments of the rescaled GOE counting measure on any compact interval are bounded uniformly in the matrix dimension.

        "exact_quote": "There is an integer $\\ell\\ge1$, depending only on $b,J$, with the following properties. Given any finite order $p_*>0$ and a sufficiently small prescribed number $\\delta_{\\rm tree}>0$, one can choose $h,w_0>0$ so that, uniformly for $e\\in J$, $0<w\\le w_0$, and $0<\\eta\\le h$, $$\\label{eq:tree-small-moments}\n \\|Y(e,\\eta)\\|_2\\le\\delta_{\\rm tree},\\qquad\n \\mathbb ED(Y(e,\\eta))^p\\le K_p\\quad(0<p\\le p_*).$$ The choices also include the additional fixed finite moment orders required by the stability assertion that follows. There are constants $\\delta_*>0$ and $C<\\infty$ such that, if a probability law $P$ on $\\mathbb D$ satisfies $\\int|x|^2\\,dP(x)\\le\\delta_*^2$ and $$W_2\\bigl(P,\\mathop{\\mathrm{Law}}(F_j(X_1,\\ldots,X_{b^j}))\\bigr)\\le u,\n \\qquad j=1,\\ell,$$ where the $X_i$ are independent with law $P$, then $$\\label{eq:tree-law-stability}\n W_2(P,\\mathop{\\mathrm{Law}}(Y(e,\\eta)))\\le Cu.$$ The constants $K_p,\\delta_*,C$ can be fixed before decreasing the parameters further to make $\\delta_{\\rm tree}$ much smaller than $\\delta_*$. On a fixed tree with its fixed potential variables, $(e,\\eta)\\mapsto Y(e,\\eta)$ extends continuously in $L^2$ to $J\\times[0,h]$. The boundary values belong to $\\mathbb D$ almost surely, obey <a href=\"#eq:tree-small-moments\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:tree-small-moments\">[eq:tree-small-moments]</a>, and are measurable functions of the potentials.",
        "source_id": "PM-219.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-tree-log",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a finite exponent $p_0\\ge1$ before choosing the disorder threshold. Taking the finite order $p_*$ in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:tree-stability\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:tree-stability\">2</a> sufficiently large, the full-root Green function and every entry of the star Green matrix extend continuously in $L^{p_0}$ to $J\\times[0,h]$ on the common environment. Their boundary values have strictly positive root imaginary part. If $K(\\zeta)$ is the imaginary part of the Green matrix compressed to an edge or a retained star, then $K(e+i0)$ is positive definite almost surely. Uniformly for $e\\in J$ and $0\\le\\eta\\le h$, the variables $\\log\\det K(e+i\\eta)$ are uniformly integrable, and they converge in $L^1$ when $(e,\\eta)$ converges. These assertions require only a finite collection of inverse-margin moments of spatial cavity variables, independent of the number of colors in a Gaussian field.",
        "source_id": "PM-219.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $H=A+\\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}(V_v)$, where $A$ is a real symmetric matrix independent of the iid uniform potentials $V_v\\in[-w,w]$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-local-consequences",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $E$ in the interior of $J$ and $s\\in\\mathbb R$. The following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *At $\\zeta=E+(s+iy)/n$, in the order $n\\to\\infty$ and then $y\\to\\infty$, the diagonal $G_{vv}(\\zeta)$ at a uniform vertex, jointly with every fixed ordered neighborhood of potentials around $v$, converges to the tree diagonal $m_o(E+i0)$ with the corresponding tree environment. For any fixed number of independently sampled vertices the joint assertion holds with independent tree environments, and their off-diagonal Green entries tend to zero in probability. The diagonal assertion also holds along every sequence $n\\eta\\to\\infty$, $(e,\\eta)\\to(E,0)$.*\n\n\n2.  *There are $c,C>0$ such that, for every sufficiently large fixed $y$, $$\\label{eq:trace-slow-power}\n     \\limsup_{n\\to\\infty}\\mathbb P\\left\\{\n     \\left|\\Im\\frac1n\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}G\\left(E+\\frac{s+iy}{n}\\right)\n                 -\\pi\\rho_{d,w}(E)\\right|>y^{-c}\\right\\}\\le Cy^{-c}.$$ The constants are independent of the fixed real number $s$.*\n\n\n3.  *For each fixed $s\\in\\mathbb R$ and $y>0$, the sampled diagonal imaginary parts are uniformly integrable in the following limiting sense: $$\\label{eq:microscopic-ui}\n     \\lim_{T\\to\\infty}\\limsup_{n\\to\\infty}\n     \\mathbb E_{H,v}\\left[\n     \\Im G_{vv}\\left(E+\\frac{s+iy}{n}\\right)\n     \\mathbf1_{\\{\\Im G_{vv}(E+(s+iy)/n)>T\\}}\\right]=0.$$*\n\n*The double-limit assertion in the first item means that, for bounded continuous tests, the limsup of the discrepancy as $n\\to\\infty$ tends to zero as $y\\to\\infty$.",
        "source_id": "PM-219.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-local-consequences",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $E$ in the interior of $J$ and $s\\in\\mathbb R$. For any fixed number of independently sampled vertices the joint assertion holds with independent tree environments, and their off-diagonal Green entries tend to zero in probability. The diagonal assertion also holds along every sequence $n\\eta\\to\\infty$, $(e,\\eta)\\to(E,0)$.*\n\n\n2.  *There are $c,C>0$ such that, for every sufficiently large fixed $y$, $$\\label{eq:trace-slow-power}\n     \\limsup_{n\\to\\infty}\\mathbb P\\left\\{\n     \\left|\\Im\\frac1n\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}G\\left(E+\\frac{s+iy}{n}\\right)\n                 -\\pi\\rho_{d,w}(E)\\right|>y^{-c}\\right\\}\\le Cy^{-c}.$$ The constants are independent of the fixed real number $s$.*\n\n\n3.  *For each fixed $s\\in\\mathbb R$ and $y>0$, the sampled diagonal imaginary parts are uniformly integrable in the following limiting sense: $$\\label{eq:microscopic-ui}\n     \\lim_{T\\to\\infty}\\limsup_{n\\to\\infty}\n     \\mathbb E_{H,v}\\left[\n     \\Im G_{vv}\\left(E+\\frac{s+iy}{n}\\right)\n     \\mathbf1_{\\{\\Im G_{vv}(E+(s+iy)/n)>T\\}}\\right]=0.$$*\n\n*The double-limit assertion in the first item means that, for bounded continuous tests, the limsup of the discrepancy as $n\\to\\infty$ tends to zero as $y\\to\\infty$.",

      "exact_statement": "Fix $E$ in the interior of $J$ and $s\\in\\mathbb R$. The following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *At $\\zeta=E+(s+iy)/n$, in the order $n\\to\\infty$ and then $y\\to\\infty$, the diagonal $G_{vv}(\\zeta)$ at a uniform vertex, jointly with every fixed ordered neighborhood of potentials around $v$, converges to the tree diagonal $m_o(E+i0)$ with the corresponding tree environment. For any fixed number of independently sampled vertices the joint assertion holds with independent tree environments, and their off-diagonal Green entries tend to zero in probability. The diagonal assertion also holds along every sequence $n\\eta\\to\\infty$, $(e,\\eta)\\to(E,0)$.*\n\n\n2.  *There are $c,C>0$ such that, for every sufficiently large fixed $y$, $$\\label{eq:trace-slow-power}\n     \\limsup_{n\\to\\infty}\\mathbb P\\left\\{\n     \\left|\\Im\\frac1n\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}G\\left(E+\\frac{s+iy}{n}\\right)\n                 -\\pi\\rho_{d,w}(E)\\right|>y^{-c}\\right\\}\\le Cy^{-c}.$$ The constants are independent of the fixed real number $s$.*\n\n\n3.  *For each fixed $s\\in\\mathbb R$ and $y>0$, the sampled diagonal imaginary parts are uniformly integrable in the following limiting sense: $$\\label{eq:microscopic-ui}\n     \\lim_{T\\to\\infty}\\limsup_{n\\to\\infty}\n     \\mathbb E_{H,v}\\left[\n     \\Im G_{vv}\\left(E+\\frac{s+iy}{n}\\right)\n     \\mathbf1_{\\{\\Im G_{vv}(E+(s+iy)/n)>T\\}}\\right]=0.$$*\n\n*The double-limit assertion in the first item means that, for bounded continuous tests, the limsup of the discrepancy as $n\\to\\infty$ tends to zero as $y\\to\\infty$.",
      "explanation": "The local law yields convergence of sampled diagonal resolvents to the tree diagonal with its environment, concentration of the normalized imaginary trace around the tree density, and microscopic uniform integrability of diagonal imaginary parts.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-marked-limits",
        "exact_quote": "Every sequence of admissible sizes tending to infinity has a subsequence on which $\\nu_n$ converges in law to a locally finite point measure $\\nu$. On a further subsequence one can retain jointly the positions and the empirical marked-neighborhood laws for the eigenvalue-ordered lists in an exhaustion by bounded intervals, together with all their finite truncations and restrictions. Repeated limiting positions retain separate marks.*\n\n\n*Almost every resulting empirical law $\\mathcal L$, with $m$ colors, is an automorphism-invariant law on the $d$-regular tree, whose potential marginal is the deterministic iid uniform law on $[-w,w]$. If $U_v\\in\\mathbb R^m$ is its mark at $v$, then $$\\label{eq:mark-normalization-equation}\n \\mathbb E_{\\mathcal L}U_oU_o^{\\mathsf T}=\\mathrm I_m,\n \\qquad\n (V_v-E)U_v+\\sum_{u\\sim v}U_u=0\\quad\\text{at every }v.$$ The empirical laws and their restrictions to smaller lists can be chosen consistently.",
        "source_id": "PM-219.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-tree-precision",
        "exact_quote": "The centered Gaussian with covariance $K_o$ is supported on the $d$-dimensional hyperplane $$\\label{eq:star-constraint}\n C:=(V_o-E)Z+\\sum_{i=1}^dX_i=0.$$ In the coordinate volume $\\delta(C)\\,dZ\\prod_i dX_i$, equivalently the volume obtained by forgetting any one neighbor, its precision quadratic form is $$\\label{eq:star-precision}\n \\sum_{i=1}^d\\frac{|X_i+g_iZ|^2}{b_i}.$$ Write $K_o^{\\rm ret}$ for its covariance in coordinates forgetting neighbor $d$. Then $$\\label{eq:star-determinant}\n \\det K_o^{\\rm ret}=|m_o|^2\\prod_{i=1}^d b_i,\n \\qquad\n h_o^{\\rm G}:=\\mathcal H(N(0,K_o^{\\rm ret}))\n =\\frac d2\\log(2\\pi\\mathrm e)+\\frac12\\sum_i\\log b_i+\\log|m_o|.$$ If $g'$ is the cavity message from the $o$-side of the edge $(o,i)$, its edge precision and determinant are $$\\begin{aligned}\n (Z,X_i)K_{oi}^{-1}(Z,X_i)^{\\mathsf T}\n &=\\frac{|X_i+g_iZ|^2}{b_i}\n       +\\frac{|Z+g'X_i|^2}{\\Im g'},\\label{eq:edge-precision}\\\\\n \\det K_{oi}&=\\frac{b_i\\Im g'}{|1-g_i g'|^2}.\n \\label{eq:edge-determinant}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular each edge covariance is positive definite.",
        "source_id": "PM-219.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "There is a deterministic $\\chi>0$ such that, almost surely, $$\\label{eq:discrepancy}\n \\nu([v,u])=\\rho(u-v)+O(R^{1-\\chi}),\n \\qquad -R\\le v\\le u\\le R,\\qquad R\\longrightarrow\\infty,$$ uniformly in $v,u$.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-potential-switch",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $k$. At site $\\alpha\\le k$, fix a measure-preserving map $T_\\alpha:[-w,w]\\to[-w,w]$ and an event $B_\\alpha$ measurable in its root-excluded tree environment. Set $$D_\\alpha=\\boldsymbol1_{B_\\alpha}\n                 \\bigl(T_\\alpha(V_\\alpha)-V_\\alpha\\bigr),\n             \\qquad D=\\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}(D_1,\\ldots,D_k).$$ Then, for every finite set of arguments in $\\mathbb H$, the imaginary parts of $$\\label{eq:potential-switch-transform}\n       m(z)-\\partial_z\\log\\det(\\mathrm I+D M_k(z))$$ have jointly the same law as the corresponding imaginary parts of $m(z)$.       },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-gaussian-insertion",
        "exact_quote": "On the preceding extension of any microscopic subsequential law, let $\\mathcal W_r$ be the upper-left $m_I$-dimensional block of an infinite raw GOE array independent of $\\mathcal E$, and set $$\\label{eq:insertion-matrix}\n H_r=D_I+s_1\\mathrm I_{m_I}+\\sqrt{s_2}\\,\\mathcal W_r.$$ Then, along $r=2^j$, the raw eigenvalue counting measures $$\\nu^{(r)}=\\sum_{j=1}^{m_I}\\delta_{\\lambda_j(H_r)}\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\\nu$$ converge in law for the local vague topology, without conditioning. Moreover $m_I=2\\rho K+O(K^{1-\\chi})$ almost surely, and the coefficient bounds <a href=\"#eq:insertion-coefficients\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:insertion-coefficients\">[eq:insertion-coefficients]</a> hold eventually almost surely.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-dbm-input",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a sequence of deterministic diagonal matrices $V$ of dimension $m\\to\\infty$ satisfies <a href=\"#eq:dbm-regularity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:dbm-regularity\">[eq:dbm-regularity]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:dbm-parameter-domain\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:dbm-parameter-domain\">[eq:dbm-parameter-domain]</a>, with fixed constants. Fix $\\sigma>0$, and suppose $$\\label{eq:dbm-time-window}\n g m^\\sigma\\le t\\le m^{-\\sigma}G^2.$$ Assume also that $\\rho_{\\mathrm{fc},t}(0)=1/\\pi$. For every fixed positive integer $a$ and $O\\in C_c^\\infty(\\mathbb R^a)$, the difference $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\label{eq:dbm-marginal-test}\n &\\int_{\\mathbb R^a}O(\\boldsymbol\\alpha)\n   p_{V+\\sqrt t\\,W_m}^{(a)}\n      \\left(\\frac{\\pi\\alpha_1}{m},\\ldots,\n            \\frac{\\pi\\alpha_a}{m}\\right)\n       \\,d\\boldsymbol\\alpha \\notag\\\\\n &\\hspace{12mm}-\n \\int_{\\mathbb R^a}O(\\boldsymbol\\alpha)\n   p_{W_m}^{(a)}\n      \\left(\\frac{\\pi\\alpha_1}{m},\\ldots,\n            \\frac{\\pi\\alpha_a}{m}\\right)\n       \\,d\\boldsymbol\\alpha\n \\longrightarrow0.\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently, the expected sums of $O$ over ordered tuples of distinct points of the two eigenvalue processes, both rescaled by $m/\\pi$, differ by $o(1)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-219.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-dbm-input",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose a sequence of deterministic diagonal matrices $V$ of dimension $m\\to\\infty$ satisfies <a href=\"#eq:dbm-regularity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:dbm-regularity\">[eq:dbm-regularity]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:dbm-parameter-domain\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:dbm-parameter-domain\">[eq:dbm-parameter-domain]</a>, with fixed constants. Fix $\\sigma>0$, and suppose $$\\label{eq:dbm-time-window}\n g m^\\sigma\\le t\\le m^{-\\sigma}G^2.$$ Assume also that $\\rho_{\\mathrm{fc},t}(0)=1/\\pi$.       "exact_statement": "Suppose a sequence of deterministic diagonal matrices $V$ of dimension $m\\to\\infty$ satisfies <a href=\"#eq:dbm-regularity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:dbm-regularity\">[eq:dbm-regularity]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:dbm-parameter-domain\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:dbm-parameter-domain\">[eq:dbm-parameter-domain]</a>, with fixed constants. Fix $\\sigma>0$, and suppose $$\\label{eq:dbm-time-window}\n g m^\\sigma\\le t\\le m^{-\\sigma}G^2.$$ Assume also that $\\rho_{\\mathrm{fc},t}(0)=1/\\pi$. For every fixed positive integer $a$ and $O\\in C_c^\\infty(\\mathbb R^a)$, the difference $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\label{eq:dbm-marginal-test}\n &\\int_{\\mathbb R^a}O(\\boldsymbol\\alpha)\n   p_{V+\\sqrt t\\,W_m}^{(a)}\n      \\left(\\frac{\\pi\\alpha_1}{m},\\ldots,\n            \\frac{\\pi\\alpha_a}{m}\\right)\n       \\,d\\boldsymbol\\alpha \\notag\\\\\n &\\hspace{12mm}-\n \\int_{\\mathbb R^a}O(\\boldsymbol\\alpha)\n   p_{W_m}^{(a)}\n      \\left(\\frac{\\pi\\alpha_1}{m},\\ldots,\n            \\frac{\\pi\\alpha_a}{m}\\right)\n       \\,d\\boldsymbol\\alpha\n \\longrightarrow0.\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently, the expected sums of $O$ over ordered tuples of distinct points of the two eigenvalue processes, both rescaled by $m/\\pi$, differ by $o(1)$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Disordered Hamiltonian** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For $w>0$, put $$H_{n,d,w}=A_{n,d}+w\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\omega_1,\ldots,\omega_n).$$
- **Normalized GOE** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Write $M_n$ for normalized GOE: its entries on and above the diagonal are independent centered real Gaussians, with variance $1/n$ off the diagonal and $2/n$ on the diagonal.
- **Unit-intensity GOE bulk reference** ([source](#pm-h-3)): If its eigenvalues are $\mu_1,\ldots,\mu_n$, the unit-intensity bulk reference at zero is $$\Xi_n^{\mathrm{GOE}}=\sum_{i=1}^n\delta_{(n/\pi)\mu_i}.$$
- **Infinite regular tree with potentials** ([source](#eq:tree-dos-definition)): Let $\mathbb T_d$ be the infinite $d$-regular tree with root $o$, equipped with independent uniform potentials $\omega_v\in[-1,1]$.
- **Potential scaling convention** ([source](#pm-h-5)): We write $V_v=w\omega_v$, so that potentials denoted by $V$ include the coupling constant and are uniform on $[-w,w]$.
- **Branching parameter b** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Set $b=d-1$.
- **Environment** ([source](#pm-h-5)): An environment consists of the potentials, including their coupling constant.
- **Forward tree** ([source](#eq:tree-recursion)): A forward tree is the component left after removing the edge from its root towards its parent.
- **Star and retained star** ([source](#pm-h-10)): By a retained star we mean the coordinate set obtained by omitting one neighbor, while keeping the root and the other $d-1$ neighbors.
- **Colors** ([source](#sec:entropy)): We call these coordinates colors.
- **Root-excluded environment** ([source](#pm-h-35)): Each sampled tree has a distinguished root; the *root-excluded environment* means all its potential coordinates other than that root coordinate.
- **Principal value convention** ([source](#stmt-lem-discrepancy)): Here and below $\mathop{\mathrm{PV}}$ means symmetric truncation in the position: $\lim_{L\to\infty}\sum_{\|x_j\|\le L}$.
- **Raw GOE** ([source](#pm-h-37)): In this section a *raw GOE* matrix has independent upper-triangular entries of variance one off the diagonal and two on the diagonal, and centered Gaussian entries throughout.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-degree-lower-bound | regular graph degree | ≥ | $3$ | fixed degree d |
| q-clean-cavity-modulus | modulus of the clean boundary solution | = | $b^{-1/2}$ | energies in the bulk interval |
| q-ward-offdiagonal-bound | mean square off-diagonal resolvent entry at independent vertices | ≤ | $\frac{C_w}{y}$ | y=n\eta |
| q-local-law-failure-probability | failure probability of the distributional local law | ≤ | $Cy^{-c}$ | uniform in energy in J and 0<eta<=h |
| q-empirical-test-discrepancy | mean square discrepancy between empirical and recursion test averages | ≤ | $Cy^{-c}$ | bounded Lipschitz tests |
| q-tree-density-formula | tree density of states in the bulk | = | $\frac1\pi\mathbb E\Im m_o(e+i0)$ | interior of J |
| q-star-covariance-determinant | determinant of the retained star covariance | = | $\|m_o\|^2\prod_{i=1}^d b_i$ | coordinates forgetting neighbor d |
| q-precision-split-identity | sum of the two endpoint precision contributions on an edge | = | $\mathrm I_{2m}$ | each environment |
| q-position-inverse-square-tail | tail of inverse squared positions | = | $O(R^{-1})$ | R tends to infinity |
| q-discrepancy-exponent-range | discrepancy exponent | < | $1/4$ | exponent may be decreased if necessary |
| q-inner-cutoff | inner spectral cutoff | = | $r^{1/100}$ | r number of sampled roots |
| q-outer-cutoff | outer spectral cutoff | = | $r^{100/\chi}$ | r number of sampled roots |
| q-inner-dimension | dimension of the inner block | = | $2\rho K+O(K^{1-\chi})$ | almost surely |
| q-schur-correction-size | weighted Schur correction norm | = | $o_{\mathbb P}(T^{-5})$ | r tends to infinity |
| q-dbm-target-density | free-convolution density at zero | = | $1/\pi$ | fixed-energy specialization at energy zero |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-tree-stability** [claim](#stmt-prop-tree-stability) / [proof](#pm-h-9): Deterministic geometry of the recursion in disk coordinates, mean and fluctuation contraction through coupling, bootstrap from smallness to moments, then continuity of boundary values. -> small boundary values with inverse-margin moments and law stability
- **step-tree-log** [claim](#stmt-lem-tree-log) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tree-log): Polynomial bounds in a margin variable for star matrices, then uniform integrability. -> positive definite covariances with integrable log-determinants
- **step-tree-density** [claim](#stmt-cor-tree-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-tree-density): Poisson kernel approximation and Fubini using continuity of the root Green function. -> continuous positive density of states
- **step-spectral-averaging** [claim](#stmt-lem-spectral-averaging) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-spectral-averaging): Rank-one spectral averaging and a harmonic-function argument for compression tails. -> averaged resolvent and count bounds
- **step-sampled-ward** [claim](#stmt-lem-ward) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ward): Ward identity for the resolvent followed by averaging over sampled vertices. -> small off-diagonal entries
- **step-empirical-recursion** [claim](#stmt-prop-empirical-recursion) / [proof](#pm-h-18): Marked boundary switches, resolvent comparison after switching, replacement of empirical potentials by iid ones, and passage from tests to transportation distance. -> approximate branch recursion for empirical laws
- **step-local-law** [claim](#stmt-prop-local-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-empirical-recursion): Start at a fixed height near the clean tree and descend through geometric heights, applying recursion and tree stability at each step. -> distributional local law
- **step-local-consequences** [claim](#stmt-cor-local-consequences) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-local-consequences): Augmented recursion for joint convergence, coupling and Markov bounds for the trace, intercept bounds and a Harnack argument for uniform integrability. -> environment convergence, trace concentration, microscopic integrability
- **step-marked-limits** [claim](#stmt-prop-marked-limits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-marked-limits): Tightness from the Wegner bound and diagonal extraction of subsequences, with second moments preserved by uniform integrability. -> marked empirical limit laws
- **step-conditional-centering** [claim](#stmt-lem-conditional-mean) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conditional-mean): Small simultaneous potential change with spectral averaging over a mesoscopic window. -> vanishing conditional mean
- **step-star-edge-precision** [claim](#stmt-lem-tree-precision) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tree-precision): Gaussian conditioning on the constraint hyperplane and direct computation of edge precision. -> explicit precision, determinant, entropy
- **step-tree-log-potential** [claim](#stmt-lem-tree-log-potential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tree-log-potential): Differentiate the recursion identity, cancel indirect terms by edge reversal, fix the constant at infinity, pass to the boundary. -> entropy constant identity
- **step-conditional-entropy** [claim](#stmt-prop-conditional-entropy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-conditional-entropy): Configuration-model label count, Gaussian smoothing, removal of binning, and monotone conditioning limits. -> entropy lower bound conditional on the environment
- **step-gaussian-marks** [claim](#stmt-prop-gaussian-marks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-gaussian-marks): Heat flow with Fisher information, re-rooting invariance, equality case with commuting projections, and ergodicity of the iid environment. -> Gaussian conditional root marks
- **step-count-discrepancy** [claim](#stmt-lem-discrepancy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-discrepancy): Grid evaluation of the Poisson transform, Borel–Cantelli, and smoothing comparison of counts. -> power-saving count discrepancy
- **step-marked-resolvent** [claim](#stmt-prop-marked-resolvent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-marked-resolvent): Convergence of centered Gaussian series, comparison at large height to identify the real constant. -> marked resolvent representation
- **step-transform-regularity** [claim](#stmt-prop-transform-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-transform-regularity): One-site resampling gives a quadratic polynomial identity, then height descent on grids with Borel–Cantelli. -> uniform transform regularity
- **step-potential-switch** [claim](#stmt-prop-potential-switch) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-potential-switch): Law preservation at finite size, determinant identity, continuous mapping, approximation of maps and events. -> switch invariance of the transform
- **step-weighted-blocks** [claim](#stmt-sw-weighted-blocks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sw-weighted-blocks): Matrix Bernstein inequality after truncation, and second-moment bound for the cross block. -> weighted block norm bounds
- **step-far-block-schur** [claim](#stmt-sw-schur) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sw-schur): Neumann expansion in weighted norms and variance computation for the Schur correction. -> negligible far block and Schur correction
- **step-trace-cut** [claim](#stmt-sw-trace-cut) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sw-trace-cut): Iterated resolvent identity at a larger height and block-inverse trace comparison. -> trace restricted to inner block
- **step-gaussian-replacement** [claim](#stmt-sw-lindeberg) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sw-lindeberg): Lindeberg replacement with third-order Taylor remainders. -> GOE noise in place of rank-one sum
- **step-gaussian-insertion** [claim](#stmt-prop-gaussian-insertion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sw-lindeberg-2): Combine the finite matrix identity, trace cut and Gaussian replacement, remove conditioning, then pass from trace limits to point measures. -> point process as limit of diagonal plus GOE eigenvalues
- **step-dbm-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-dbm-input) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-dbm-input): Specialization of a published fixed-energy theorem with a combinatorial normalization factor. -> GOE local statistics after short-time noise
- **step-density-adjustment** [claim](#stmt-lem-density-adjustment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-density-adjustment): Verify regularity from transform regularity, solve the boundary equation by Rouché, prove uniqueness, then dilate. -> data satisfying the DBM hypotheses
- **step-goe-counts** [claim](#stmt-lem-goe-counts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-goe-counts-2): Gaussian decimation to a determinantal process and a uniform bound on the projection kernel. -> uniform exponential count moments
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-goe-counts): Subsequence argument: identify the Laplace functional of every subsequential limit with GOE, then conclude for the full sequence. -> fixed-energy GOE universality

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-fixed-energy-goe-limit**: For fixed degree, fixed small disorder strength and fixed bulk energy, the Laplace functional of the rescaled eigenvalue process around that energy becomes asymptotically equal to that of the GOE bulk process as the graph size grows.
- **inv-positive-continuous-dos**: The infinite-tree density of states is continuous and strictly positive on the bulk interval, and it supplies the unfolding factor used in the rescaling.
- **inv-conditional-gaussian-root-marks**: In every admissible marked subsequential limit, the eigenvector coordinates at a sampled root are, conditionally on the tree environment, centered Gaussian with variance set by the imaginary part of the tree Green function.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-strong-disorder**: Apply the GOE bulk statement at a disorder strength above the threshold, or let the disorder grow with the graph size. -> The theorem gives no information; the smallness choices that feed the tree stability and every later step are lost.
- **anti-outside-bulk**: Use the fixed-energy statement at an energy outside the closed bulk interval, such as near the clean spectral edges. -> The tree density and its strict positivity are only established inside the bulk interval, so the unfolding and the conclusion are not covered.
- **anti-low-degree**: Use the result for degree one or two graphs such as paths or cycles. -> The proofs use branching of the tree and degree at least three, so the statement is not available.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is an asymptotic statement about a limit as the graph size tends to infinity, proved through subsequential compactness, entropy equality and Dyson Brownian motion inputs. No finite procedure with a computable output is given, so no runnable reference implementation or exact oracle exists.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves an asymptotic universality theorem for eigenvalue statistics. It gives no algorithm, API or data format that a program could implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result concerns limits as the graph size tends to infinity. It supplies no discretisation or time-stepping scheme for a simulator. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No checker or generator is constructed. The statements are existence and limit assertions, and the disorder threshold is not given as a computable quantity. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof is analytic: tree stability, entropy rigidity, Gaussian insertion and a Dyson Brownian motion input. It does not describe a finite procedure with a stated running time. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data structure appears. Graph switches and stub matchings are proof devices that preserve a probability law, not a representation to implement. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns arithmetic kernels, memory layouts or hardware. The estimates are qualitative power-law bounds. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, protocol or security reduction is involved. Spectral universality is not a source of pseudorandomness guarantees. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning setting, loss or training procedure appears. The random matrices here are theoretical objects with fixed degree and weak disorder. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective or constraint structure is optimised. Entropy inequalities are used only for identification of a limit law. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Random regular graphs could model overlay networks, but the paper states nothing about protocols, latency or fault tolerance, so any use would be an unsupported analogy. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The natural home is mathematical physics of disordered operators, which is a theoretical use rather than an engineering construction. The theorem gives no explicit threshold. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **lim-weak-disorder-threshold** (hypothesis): The universality statement is proved only for a fixed disorder strength below a threshold that depends on the degree and on the distance from the clean spectral edges. The paper gives no explicit value for this threshold. Blocked use: ['applying the GOE bulk statement at a disorder strength above the threshold', 'letting the disorder grow with the graph size', 'computing a numerical admissible disorder from this paper alone']
- **lim-fixed-degree-three** (hypothesis): The degree is a fixed integer, at least three, and does not vary with the graph size. The tree arguments use branching and the star geometry, which need degree at least three. Blocked use: ['using the result for paths or cycles of degree one or two', 'letting the degree grow with the graph size']
- **lim-bulk-interval-only** (edge_case): All smallness choices are made uniformly for energies in a compact interval inside the clean band. The conclusion is therefore restricted to energies in that bulk subinterval, at positive distance from the clean spectral edges. Blocked use: ['using the fixed-energy statement at or near the clean spectral edges', 'using the bulk statement with a margin that shrinks with the graph size']
- **lim-ineffective-constants** (approximation): The finite-graph estimates carry constants that depend on the fixed positive disorder strength, and the local-law exponents may be decreased as the argument proceeds. No quantitative rate is available for the final convergence. Blocked use: ['quoting a convergence rate or a finite-size error bar for the eigenvalue statistics', 'choosing a graph size that guarantees a given accuracy']
- **lim-subsequence-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): The microscopic limit laws are obtained by compactness and diagonal extraction along subsequences. The conclusion for the full sequence follows only by a subsequence argument, with no explicit procedure. Blocked use: ['reading the proof as an algorithm that computes the limit process', 'extracting an effective construction of subsequential limits']
- **lim-dbm-density-normalization** (formal_scope): The Dyson Brownian motion input requires deterministic diagonal data that are regular in a stated parameter window, a time window, and free-convolution density exactly the reference value at the energy. The paper must adjust the data by a dilation to meet this normalization. Blocked use: ['applying the DBM input to diagonal data that are not regular in the stated window', 'omitting the density normalization when unfolding the spectrum']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every fixed degree <i>d</i> ≥ 3, we prove fixed-energy GOE universality for the adjacency matrix of a uniformly random simple labelled <i>d</i>-regular graph with independent uniform diagonal disorder. The disorder strength is positive, sufficiently small, and fixed as the graph grows. At each fixed energy in a compact subinterval of the clean spectral band, the full microscopic eigenvalue point process converges to the GOE bulk process after rescaling by the positive density of states of the corresponding infinite-tree operator. The permitted disorder strength depends on the degree and the distance from the clean spectral edges.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-thm-main](#stmt-thm-main) | theorem | `thm:main` | 1 | For every $d\ge3$ and every $0<\kappa<2\sqrt{d-1}$, there is $w_0=w_0(d,\kappa)>0$ with the following property. |
| [stmt-prop-tree-stability](#stmt-prop-tree-stability) | proposition | `prop:tree-stability` | 2 | There is an integer $\ell\ge1$, depending only on $b,J$, with the following properties. |
| [stmt-lem-tree-log](#stmt-lem-tree-log) | lemma | `lem:tree-log` | 3 | Fix a finite exponent $p_0\ge1$ before choosing the disorder threshold. |
| [stmt-cor-tree-density](#stmt-cor-tree-density) | corollary | `cor:tree-density` | 4 | For the above smallness choices, the restriction of $\nu_{d,w}$ to the interior of $J$ has the continuous strictly positive density |
